Moreover, it was shown in [22] that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor \cdot \rfloor : \mathcal {K}^{n+1} \rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}⌊·⌋:Kn+1→Convcd(Rn) is continuous and onto.

Note that the space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Convcd(Rn) can be identified with a subset of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R) by taking the convex conjugate (Legendre transform), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^*(x) = \sup _{y \in \mathbb {R}^n} \langle x, y \rangle - f(y)$$\end{document}f∗(x)=supy∈Rn⟨x,y⟩-f(y). Indeed, the space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R) is mapped by the Legendre transform to the space\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{sc}}(\mathbb {R}^n) = \{f: \mathbb {R}^n \rightarrow (-\infty ,\infty ]:\,&\text {{ f} is convex, lower semi-continuous},\\ &f \not \equiv +\infty , \text { and } \lim \limits _{\Vert x\Vert \rightarrow \infty }\frac{f(x)}{\Vert x\Vert } = \infty \}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Convsc(Rn)={f:Rn→(-∞,∞]:f is convex, lower semi-continuous,f≢+∞,andlim‖x‖→∞f(x)‖x‖=∞},of super-coercive convex functions. Clearly, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n) \subset \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{sc}}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Convcd(Rn)⊂Convsc(Rn) and, thus, its image under the the Legendre transform satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*\subset \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Convcd(Rn)∗⊂Conv(Rn,R). This subset is dense ([22, Cor. 2.3]) and the following extension property holds.

Theorem 5.2

([42]) Suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu : \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}μ:Convcd(Rn)∗→R is a continuous, dually epi-translation-invariant valuation. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ extends uniquely by continuity to a continuous, dually epi-translation-invariant valuation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\mu }: \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R}) \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}μ^:Conv(Rn,R)→R.

Let us further point out that for an affine function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda = \langle v, \cdot \rangle + c$$\end{document}λ=⟨v,·⟩+c, its convex conjugate is given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda ^*(x) = \mathbbm {1}_{\{v\}}^\infty - c = \lfloor \{(v,-c)\}\rfloor $$\end{document}λ∗(x)=1{v}∞-c=⌊{(v,-c)}⌋, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbbm {1}^\infty _K$$\end{document}1K∞ denotes the convex indicator function of K taking the value 0 in K and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\infty $$\end{document}∞ outside.

We are now ready to prove Theorem C.

Proof of Theorem C

Suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi : \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R}) \rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ:Conv(Rn,R)→Conv(0)(Rn) is a continuous and dually epi-translation-invariant valuation such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (0) \in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ(0)∈Conv(Rn,R).

First, consider \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\Psi }:\mathcal {K}^{n+1}\rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ~:Kn+1→Conv(0)(Rn) given by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\Psi }(K):=\Psi (\lfloor K \rfloor ^*)$$\end{document}Ψ~(K):=Ψ(⌊K⌋∗), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \in \mathcal {K}^{n+1}$$\end{document}K∈Kn+1. Then, by the continuity of the Legendre transform and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor \cdot \rfloor $$\end{document}⌊·⌋, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\Psi }$$\end{document}Ψ~ is a continuous map and as the Legendre transform exchanges minimum and maximum, it is also a valuation, compare [22, Thm. 9]. Consequently, Lemma 5.1 implies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} U\subset {{\,\textrm{dom}\,}}\tilde{\Psi }(K)={{\,\textrm{dom}\,}}\Psi (\lfloor K\rfloor ^*)\quad \text {for all}~K\in \mathcal {K}^{n+1}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}U⊂domΨ~(K)=domΨ(⌊K⌋∗)for allK∈Kn+1,where U is the largest open set contained in all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{dom}\,}}\tilde{\Psi }(\{(x,t)\})$$\end{document}domΨ~({(x,t)}), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,t) \in \mathbb {R}^{n+1}$$\end{document}(x,t)∈Rn+1. By the remark above the proof, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor (x,t) \rfloor = \lambda ^*$$\end{document}⌊(x,t)⌋=λ∗ for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda (y) = \langle x, y \rangle -t$$\end{document}λ(y)=⟨x,y⟩-t. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\Psi }(\{(x,t)\}) = \Psi (\lambda ) = \Psi (0)$$\end{document}Ψ~({(x,t)})=Ψ(λ)=Ψ(0) for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(x,t) \in \mathbb {R}^{n+1}$$\end{document}(x,t)∈Rn+1, as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi $$\end{document}Ψ is dually epi-translation-invariant.

We conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U = \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}U=Rn. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lfloor \cdot \rfloor :\mathcal {K}^{n+1}\rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}⌊·⌋:Kn+1→Convcd(Rn) is onto, this implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (f)$$\end{document}Ψ(f) is finite for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*$$\end{document}f∈Convcd(Rn)∗. By Theorem 5.2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi _x|_{\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*}$$\end{document}Ψx|Convcd(Rn)∗ extends uniquely by continuity to a real-valued (that is, finite) valuation on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R) for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}x∈Rn, which we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\Psi }_x$$\end{document}Ψ^x. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}f∈Conv(Rn,R) define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\Psi }(f):\mathbb {R}^n\rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}Ψ^(f):Rn→R by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\Psi }(f)[x]:=\widehat{\Psi }_x(f)$$\end{document}Ψ^(f)[x]:=Ψ^x(f). Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\mapsto \widehat{\Psi }(f)$$\end{document}f↦Ψ^(f) is continuous with respect to pointwise convergence. Since this function is convex for functions belonging to the dense subspace \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*$$\end{document}Convcd(Rn)∗ of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R) and pointwise limits of convex functions are again convex, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\Psi }(f)\in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ^(f)∈Conv(Rn,R) for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}f∈Conv(Rn,R). By [48, Thm. 7.58] the topology induced by epi-convergence coincides with the topology induced by pointwise convergence on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R). In particular, we may consider this as a continuous map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\Psi }:\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})\rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ^:Conv(Rn,R)→Conv(0)(Rn). Since this map coincides with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi $$\end{document}Ψ on the dense subspace \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*$$\end{document}Convcd(Rn)∗, these two maps have to coincide on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R). In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (f)\in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ(f)∈Conv(Rn,R) for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}f∈Conv(Rn,R). This concludes the proof of the first statement.

It remains to prove the second statement for equivariant valuations. To this end, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi : \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R}) \rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ:Conv(Rn,R)→Conv(0)(Rn) be a continuous, dually epi-translation-invariant valuation which is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{SL}\,}}(n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}SL(n,R)-equi- resp. contravariant. By the equi- resp. contravariance, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (0)$$\end{document}Ψ(0) is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{SL}\,}}(n,\mathbb {R})$$\end{document}SL(n,R)-invariant. As \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (0)\in \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ(0)∈Conv(0)(Rn) is finite in a neighborhood of the origin, it must be constant and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\,\textrm{dom}\,}}\Psi (0) = \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}domΨ(0)=Rn. Hence, the first part of the proof implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})) \subset \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ(Conv(Rn,R))⊂Conv(Rn,R) and an application of Theorem A resp. B concludes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Note that we actually proved the following statement.

Proposition 5.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi : \textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*\rightarrow \textrm{Conv}_{(0)}(\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Ψ:Convcd(Rn)∗→Conv(0)(Rn) be a continuous valuation. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (h) \in \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ(h)∈Conv(Rn,R) for every affine h, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Psi (\textrm{Conv}_{\textrm{cd}}(\mathbb {R}^n)^*) \subset \textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Ψ(Convcd(Rn)∗)⊂Conv(Rn,R).

The condition of dual epi-translation-invariance in Theorem C was only needed in the last step to lift the statement to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Conv}(\mathbb {R}^n, \mathbb {R})$$\end{document}Conv(Rn,R).
